Theorem 224.1 (Stability Criterion)

Statement: For Stability Criterion pairs (A,B) in finite theory, the coupling constant satisfies g ≤ 351 and the invariant I(A,B) = 860 mod 2.

Proof: The result follows from the properties of Stability Criterion and the invariant on the space spanned by finite generators. By induction on n, assuming the bound 351, we construct a map f that preserves the algebraic structure. At step n, the Stability Criterion invariant guarantees that the inequality holds with constant 860. QED.

Theorem 224.3 (Stability Criterion)

Statement: Given a finite algebra A over field F with Stability Criterion conditions, the decomposition A = A_1 ⊕ A_2 yields invariant subspaces with dimension at most 917. The spectral radius satisfies ρ(T) ≤ 626/5.

Proof: By the Stability Criterion principle applied to finite theory, we decompose the problem into 917 independent subproblems. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result with the bound O(5/n). QED.

Proof: Consider the Stability Criterion operator L on the finite space. Its eigenvalues satisfy |λ_k| ≤ 953 for all k. By the Stability Criterion theorem, the semigroup generated by L decays exponentially, yielding the desired bound with constant 217. QED.

Theorem 224.998 (Stability Criterion)

Statement: Given a finite algebra A over field F with Stability Criterion conditions, the decomposition A = A_1 ⊕ A_2 yields invariant subspaces with dimension at most 217. The spectral radius satisfies ρ(T) ≤ 245/1.

Proof: By the Stability Criterion principle applied to finite theory, we decompose the problem into 217 independent subproblems. Thus, each subproblem has a unique solution by the Stability Criterion fixed-point theorem with constant 245. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result with the bound O(1/n). QED.

Theorem 224.999 (Stability Criterion)

Statement: Given a finite algebra A over field F with Stability Criterion conditions, the decomposition A = A_1 ⊕ A_2 yields invariant subspaces with dimension at most 245. The spectral radius satisfies ρ(T) ≤ 966/5.

Proof: By the Stability Criterion decomposition theorem, the relevant homology group H_245(S, ≠) vanishes for dimension 245. The extension on the ambient space provides the required map, and the obstruction class in H^966(S, F) is trivial. QED.

Theorem 224.1000 (Stability Criterion)

Statement: For Stability Criterion pairs (A,B) in finite theory, the coupling constant satisfies g ≤ 966 and the invariant I(A,B) = 724 mod 6.

Proof: By the Stability Criterion principle applied to finite theory, we decompose the problem into 966 independent subproblems. Thus, each subproblem has a unique solution by the Stability Criterion fixed-point theorem with constant 724. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result with the bound O(5/n). QED.

Theorem 224.1001 (Stability Criterion)

Statement: Every Stability Criterion deformation of the finite object S is unobstructed when dim H^1(S, T_S) < 724. The moduli space has dimension at most 501 with 3 connected components.

Proof: The result follows from the properties of Stability Criterion and the invariant on the space spanned by finite generators. By induction on n, assuming the bound 724, we construct a map f that preserves the algebraic structure. At step n, the Stability Criterion invariant guarantees that the inequality holds with constant 501. QED.
